````
1	# Language Model
2	
3	How Half-Life asks Apple's on-device language model for text, the tools the model can call, and when the features that use it are hidden.
4	
5	## Overview
6	
7	The Foundation Models layer (roadmap rank 11) gives other features one way to ask Apple's on-device language model for text. Three roadmap items build on it: insight cards (rank 12), App Intents (rank 14), which carry the brief's "text the app" bonus, and the Insights tab (rank 15). None of them is built yet, so nothing on screen shows the model's text yet.
8	
9	**The rules calculate, and the model only phrases.** The model never works out a number. Every amount, time, and drink it mentions comes from a tool, and each tool reads it from the same repository the screens read. An answer can't disagree with the Today screen.
10	
11	### Decisions
12	
13	The owner settled these on 2026-09-12.
14	
15	| Decision | Why |
16	|----------|-----|
17	| Tools read repositories, never data sources. | Constitution Article V.3.5 lets only repositories reach HealthKit data sources, and Article I.10 has repositories execute the business rules. A tool that read a data source would have to re-run the rules, and could disagree with the screen. |
18	| Tools only read, except `logDrink`, which logs a drink through ``LogDrinkUseCase``. | Logging goes through the same use case as the composer, one-tap favourites, and App Intents, so it gets the same checks. |
19	| Only instructions from App Intents get `logDrink`. | The model can log a drink only when the user asked for one, in their own words. Text the app writes, such as a request for an insight, can never log a drink. |
20	| Each session handles a single instruction. | Half-Life isn't a chatbot. Each instruction gets a new session and new tools, which are discarded with its response. Nothing carries from one instruction to the next, and nothing is persisted. |
21	| Features that use the model are hidden while it's unavailable. | The app works fully without Apple Intelligence, as it does without Health (Article V.3.3). |
22	| `compareCaffeineTiming` reports the finding that comes with the insight's request, rather than reading a repository. The owner decided this on 2026-09-13. | The card's feature already holds the finding, for "Feel right?", so the model can't describe newer data than the card shows, and this layer doesn't reach the Sleep screen's repository. |
23	| The data source is `LanguageModelDataSource`, implemented by `FoundationModelLanguageModelDataSource`. | The owner chose the singular, although the framework is `FoundationModels`. The files live in `Data/DataSources/FoundationModels/`, named for the framework. |
24	
25	## How an instruction runs
26	
27	```
28	Feature or App Intent ─▶ RespondToInstructionUseCase ─▶ LanguageModelRepository.respond(to:)
29	  ─▶ LanguageModelDataSource.respond(to:)
30	       ─▶ a new LanguageModelSession(tools:instructions:) ─▶ one response, then discarded
31	               │
32	               ├─▶ read-only tools ─▶ CaffeineDecayRepository, DrinkLogRepository, CurrentTimeRepository
33	               └─▶ logDrink (App Intents only) ─▶ LogDrinkUseCase ─▶ DrinkLogRepository
34	```
35	
36	- **The response is returned, not published.** The repository doesn't keep it, so it isn't repository-owned data, and returning it isn't an exception to constitution Article I.5's one-way flow. A drink that `logDrink` stores does flow the usual way: the drink log data source signals the change, and the Today screen, the curve, and the favourites follow.
37	- **The tools are part of the data source, and they read repositories.** A tool conforms to the framework's `Tool` protocol, and only data sources import `FoundationModels` (Article I.14). Tools read the Domain repository protocols, never another data source. This is the only data source that reads repositories.
38	- **Each instruction builds its own tools**, so the ``LogDrinkUseCase`` that `logDrink` holds lives for a single instruction (Article I.8).
39	
40	```
````

8	//
9	// Half-Life LanguageModelInstructions
10	//
11	
12	import Foundation
13	
14	/// The standing rules every language model session starts with (LMSRC-3).
15	///
16	/// They're prompt text for the model, not text the user sees, so they're written here in English rather than in the
17	/// String Catalog. The model's answer is what the user sees, and the rules ask for the user's language.
18	enum LanguageModelInstructions {
19	    /// The instructions for a session.
20	    ///
21	    /// - Parameters:
22	    ///   - origin: Where the session's instruction came from. Only an App Intent's session is told how to log drinks.
23	    ///   - locale: The user's locale, whose language the model answers in.
24	    static func text(for origin: LanguageModelInstruction.Origin, locale: Locale) -> String {
25	        var rules = [
26	            "You write short answers for Half-Life, an app that shows how much caffeine is in the user's body and when "
27	                + "it wears off.",
28	            "Get every number, time, and drink from a tool. Never calculate, estimate, or invent one.",
29	            "Repeat amounts and times exactly as the tools give them.",
30	            "If a tool says something isn't available, say so plainly.",
31	            "Don't diagnose, and don't give medical advice.",
32	            "Answer in three sentences or fewer.",
33	            "Answer in \(languageName(for: locale)).",
34	        ]
35	        if origin == .appIntent {
36	            rules.append("Log a drink only when the user says they drank it.")
37	        }
38	        return rules.joined(separator: "\n")
39	    }
40	
```

8	//
9	// Half-Life LanguageModelFormat
10	//
11	
12	import Foundation
13	
14	/// Formats the amounts, times, days, and drinks that tools give the language model (LMTOOL-8).
15	///
16	/// The model copies what the tools give it, so it never formats or rounds a number itself. Amounts and times follow
17	/// the calendar's locale and time zone (constitution Article VII.3). Drink names are prompt text for the model, not
18	/// text the user sees, so they're in English, like the rest of the prompt.
19	struct LanguageModelFormat: Sendable {
20	    /// The calendar whose locale and time zone everything is formatted in.
21	    let calendar: Calendar
22	
23	    private var locale: Locale {
24	        calendar.locale ?? .autoupdatingCurrent
25	    }
26	
27	    /// A whole number of milligrams, such as "85 mg".
28	    func milligrams(_ value: Double) -> String {
29	        Measurement(value: value.rounded(), unit: UnitMass.milligrams)
30	            .formatted(
31	                Measurement<UnitMass>.FormatStyle(
32	                    width: .abbreviated, locale: locale, usage: .asProvided,
33	                    numberFormatStyle: .number.precision(.fractionLength(0))))
34	    }
35	
36	    /// A time of day, such as "3:05 PM".
37	    func time(_ date: Date) -> String {
38	        date.formatted(
39	            Date.FormatStyle(
40	                date: .omitted, time: .shortened, locale: locale, calendar: calendar, timeZone: calendar.timeZone))
```

12	import Foundation
13	import OSLog
14	
15	/// The live language model repository.
16	///
17	/// It holds only the availability. It re-reads it from the data source at each whole minute the clock streams,
18	/// because the framework doesn't announce changes, and sends each subscriber a new value only when it changes. It
19	/// keeps nothing from an instruction or its response. It's an actor off the main actor, like every repository
20	/// (constitution Article I.13). The Language Model article lists its requirements, LMREPO-1 to LMREPO-5.
21	actor LiveLanguageModelRepository: LanguageModelRepository {
22	    private static let logger = Logger(for: LiveLanguageModelRepository.self)
23	
24	    /// The data source that wraps the model. It never changes, so any context can read it.
25	    nonisolated let dataSource: any LanguageModelDataSource
26	    private let clock: any ClockDataSource
27	
28	    /// Creates the repository.
29	    ///
30	    /// - Parameters:
31	    ///   - dataSource: The data source that wraps the model.
32	    ///   - clock: The clock whose minutes the availability is re-read at.
33	    init(dataSource: any LanguageModelDataSource, clock: any ClockDataSource) {
34	        self.dataSource = dataSource
35	        self.clock = clock
36	    }

import Foundation

struct InsightFeedback: Sendable, Equatable {
    /// What the user answered.
    enum Answer: Sendable, Equatable {
        /// "Yes": the finding feels right.
        case agree
        /// "Not really": it doesn't.
        case disagree
    }

    /// What the user answered.
    let answer: Answer
    /// The finding's confidence when the user answered.
    let confidence: SleepPattern.Confidence
    /// The headline the user answered.
    let headline: String
    /// The sentence the user answered.
    let sentence: String
    /// When the user answered.
    let answeredAt: Date
}

struct LanguageModelInstruction: Sendable, Equatable {
    /// Where an instruction came from, which decides the tools its session gets.
    enum Origin: Sendable, Equatable {
        /// The words the user sent through an App Intent. Only these sessions can log a drink.
        case appIntent
        /// Text the app wrote, such as a request for an insight. These sessions can only read.
        case app
    }

    /// What the model is asked.
    let prompt: String
    /// Where the instruction came from.
    let origin: Origin
}

```
import Foundation

struct DrinkLogRule: Sendable {
    /// Why a drink can't be logged.
    enum Violation: Error, Equatable {
        /// The quantity is below 1.
        case quantityBelowOne
        /// The drink was consumed later than the current time.
        case consumedInFuture
    }

    /// Checks that `drink` may be logged at `now`. The quantity is checked first.
    ///
    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - drink: The drink to check.
    ///   - now: The current time.
    /// - Throws: The ``Violation`` that stops the drink from being logged.
    func validate(_ drink: LoggedDrink, now: Date) throws {
        guard drink.quantity >= 1 else { throw Violation.quantityBelowOne }
        guard drink.consumedAt <= now else { throw Violation.consumedInFuture }
    }

**15:25:02 · Tool call: Write**

- `file_path`: `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-adamure-Development-Femmli-Half-Life/ccfc6117-8989-40ec-be60-db79106e722e/scratchpad/fmaudit/main.swift`

```
// Token audit for Half-Life's FoundationModels usage.
// Mirrors the app's instructions, tool names, descriptions, and argument schemas verbatim, so the counts match.
import Foundation
import FoundationModels

// MARK: - Mirrors of the app's @Generable types

@Generable(description: "A drink from Half-Life's catalog.")
enum GeneratedDrinkType: CaseIterable {
    case espresso, americano, latte, cappuccino, flatWhite, dripCoffee, coldBrew, instantCoffee, blackTea, greenTea,
        matcha, cola, energyDrink
}

@Generable(description: "The one finding on the user's Insights card.")
struct GeneratedInsight {
    @Guide(description: "A headline of at most 8 words.")
    let headline: String
    @Guide(description: "One sentence of at most 25 words.")
    let sentence: String
}

// MARK: - Mirrors of the app's tools (same name, description, arguments; canned output)

nonisolated(unsafe) var drinksPerDay = 6

func drinkLines(_ n: Int, day: String) -> String {
    let times = ["7:05 AM", "8:40 AM", "10:45 AM", "12:30 PM", "2:15 PM", "3:50 PM", "5:20 PM", "7:10 PM"]
    let drinks = ["latte, 2 shots, 128 mg", "cold brew, 1 cup, 205 mg", "espresso, 2 shots, 128 mg",
                  "black tea, 1 cup, 47 mg", "energy drink, 1 can, 160 mg"]
    var lines = ["Drinks logged on \(day):"]
    for i in 0..<n { lines.append("- \(times[i % times.count]): \(drinks[i % drinks.count]) (demo data)") }
    lines.append("Total: 812 mg.")
    return lines.joined(separator: "\n")
}

struct CaffeineStatusTool: Tool {
    @Generable struct Arguments {}
    let name = "getCaffeineStatus"
    let description = """
        Gets the caffeine in the user's body now, how much will be left at their bedtime, and when their last drink \
        is half gone.
        """
    static let output = """
        Caffeine in the body now: 85 mg.
        At bedtime, 10:30 PM: 34 mg.
        The last drink is half gone at 4:27 PM.
        """
    func call(arguments: Arguments) async throws -> String { Self.output }
}

struct CaffeineCutoffTool: Tool {
    @Generable struct Arguments {}
    let name = "getCaffeineCutoff"
    let description = """
        Gets the latest time today the user can have their usual drink and still have little enough caffeine left at \
        bedtime to sleep, or says there's no time left today.
        """
    static let output =
        "The latest time to have the usual drink (latte, 2 shots) and still be at or under 50 mg at bedtime, 10:30 PM, is 2:30 PM."
    func call(arguments: Arguments) async throws -> String { Self.output }
}

struct CaffeineLevelTool: Tool {
    @Generable struct Arguments {
        @Guide(description: "The hour, on a 24-hour clock, from 0 to 23.", .range(0...23))
        let hour: Int
        @Guide(description: "The minute, from 0 to 59.", .range(0...59))
        let minute: Int
    }
    let name = "getCaffeineLevelAt"
    let description = """
        Gets how much caffeine will be in the user's body the next time the clock shows a given time, up to 12 hours \
        ahead.
        """
    static let output = "Caffeine in the body at 11:00 PM: 31 mg."
    func call(arguments: Arguments) async throws -> String { Self.output }
}

struct DrinksOnDayTool: Tool {
    @Generable struct Arguments {
        @Guide(description: "How many days before today: 0 is today, and 1 is yesterday.", .range(0...90))
        let daysAgo: Int
    }
    let name = "getDrinksOnDay"
    let description = "Gets every drink the user logged on one day, with its time and caffeine, and the day's total."
    func call(arguments: Arguments) async throws -> String {
        drinkLines(drinksPerDay, day: "Wednesday, September \(max(1, 13 - arguments.daysAgo))")
    }
}

struct LogDrinkTool: Tool {
    @Generable struct Arguments {
        @Guide(description: "The drink the user had.")
        let drink: GeneratedDrinkType
        @Guide(
            description: "How many servings: shots for espresso drinks, cups for brewed coffee and tea, cans for cola.",
            .range(1...10))
        let quantity: Int
        @Guide(description: "How many minutes ago the user had it: 0 is now.", .range(0...720))
        let minutesAgo: Int
    }
    let name = "logDrink"
    let description = "Logs a drink the user says they had, and reports what was logged."
    static let output = "Logged cold brew, 1 cup, 205 mg, at 3:05 PM."
    func call(arguments: Arguments) async throws -> String { Self.output }
}

struct CaffeineTimingTool: Tool {
    @Generable struct Arguments {}
    let name = "compareCaffeineTiming"
    let description = """
        Compares the user's time asleep on the nights they fell asleep with more caffeine in their body than their \
        sleep threshold, against the other nights, over the last 30 days.
        """
    static let output = """
        Period: the last 30 days, Aug 14 to Sep 12.
        Caffeine when you fell asleep: over 50 mg on 11 nights, 50 mg or under on 16 nights.
        Nights counted: 27.
        Time asleep: shorter on the nights over 50 mg than on the others.
        Confidence: consistent so far.
        3 nights aren't counted: no sleep was recorded, or no drinks were logged before them yet.
        """
    func call(arguments: Arguments) async throws -> String { Self.output }
}

// MARK: - Mirrors of LanguageModelInstructions

func instructions(appIntent: Bool) -> String {
    var rules = [
        "You write short answers for Half-Life, an app that shows how much caffeine is in the user's body and when "
            + "it wears off.",
        "Get every number, time, and drink from a tool. Never calculate, estimate, or invent one.",
        "Repeat amounts and times exactly as the tools give them.",
        "If a tool says something isn't available, say so plainly.",
        "Don't diagnose, and don't give medical advice.",
        "Answer in three sentences or fewer.",
        "Answer in English.",
    ]
    if appIntent { rules.append("Log a drink only when the user says they drank it.") }
    return rules.joined(separator: "\n")
}

let insightRules = [
    "You're writing the one finding on the user's Insights card, from the compareCaffeineTiming tool.",
    "Write a headline of at most 8 words, and one sentence of at most 25 words.",
    "Say what the tool found about the user's nights, and how many nights it's based on.",
    "Use the tool's confidence words exactly: \"an early sign\" or \"consistent so far\".",
    "Describe a pattern in the user's nights. Never say caffeine caused, cost, or took away sleep, and never say "
        + "how much sleep the user lost.",
    "Don't give advice, and don't tell the user what to do.",
    "If the user disagreed with an earlier finding, take a different approach from that one.",
]
let insightInstructions = ([instructions(appIntent: false)] + insightRules).joined(separator: "\n")

let insightPromptPlain = "Write today's finding for the user's Insights card."
let insightPromptDisagree = """
    \(insightPromptPlain)

    The user disagreed with this earlier finding:
    Headline: "Late caffeine nights run a little shorter"
    Sentence: "On the 11 nights you fell asleep over 50 mg, your time asleep was shorter than on the other 16, which is consistent so far."
    Take a different approach from the one in that finding.
    """

// MARK: - Measurement

@main
struct Audit {
    static func main() async throws {
        let model = SystemLanguageModel.default
        print("availability:", model.availability)
        print("contextSize:", model.contextSize)
        print("supportsLocale(en_US):", model.supportsLocale(Locale(identifier: "en_US")))

        func t(_ s: String) async throws -> Int { try await model.tokenCount(for: s) }
        func ti(_ s: String) async throws -> Int { try await model.tokenCount(for: Instructions(s)) }

        let qaTools: [any Tool] = [CaffeineStatusTool(), CaffeineCutoffTool(), CaffeineLevelTool(), DrinksOnDayTool(),
                                   LogDrinkTool()]
        let appTools: [any Tool] = Array(qaTools.prefix(4))
        let insightTools: [any Tool] = [CaffeineTimingTool()]

        print("\n== Fixed parts ==")
        print("instructions(appIntent):", try await ti(instructions(appIntent: true)))
        print("instructions(app):", try await ti(instructions(appIntent: false)))
        print("instructions(insight):", try await ti(insightInstructions))
        print("tools(appIntent, 5):", try await model.tokenCount(for: qaTools))
        print("tools(app, 4):", try await model.tokenCount(for: appTools))
        print("tools(insight, 1):", try await model.tokenCount(for: insightTools))
        for tool in qaTools + insightTools {
            print("  tool \(tool.name):", try await model.tokenCount(for: [tool]))
        }
        print("schema(GeneratedInsight):", try await model.tokenCount(for: GeneratedInsight.generationSchema))
        print("prompt(insight):", try await t(insightPromptPlain))
        print("prompt(insight + disagreement):", try await t(insightPromptDisagree))

        print("\n== Tool outputs ==")
        print("getCaffeineStatus:", try await t(CaffeineStatusTool.output))
        print("getCaffeineCutoff:", try await t(CaffeineCutoffTool.output))
        print("getCaffeineLevelAt:", try await t(CaffeineLevelTool.output))
        print("logDrink:", try await t(LogDrinkTool.output))
        print("compareCaffeineTiming:", try await t(CaffeineTimingTool.output))
        for n in [0, 1, 6, 12, 25, 50] {
            print("getDrinksOnDay(\(n) drinks):", try await t(drinkLines(n, day: "Wednesday, September 9")))
        }

        print("\n== User question ==")
        let shortQ = "How much caffeine will I have at bedtime?"
        print("short question:", try await t(shortQ))
        let para = String(repeating: "I had a coffee this morning and then another one after lunch and I'm wondering. ", count: 10)
        print("chars \(para.count):", try await t(para))

        guard case .available = model.availability else {
            print("\nModel unavailable here: skipping live sessions.")
            return
        }

        print("\n== Live sessions (transcript tokens after the response) ==")
        func run(_ label: String, instructions: String, tools: [any Tool], prompt: String) async {
            let session = LanguageModelSession(tools: tools, instructions: instructions)
            do {
                let r = try await session.respond(to: prompt)
                let total = try await model.tokenCount(for: session.transcript)
                let out = try await model.tokenCount(for: r.content)
                print("\(label): transcript=\(total) answer=\(out) entries=\(session.transcript.count)")
            } catch {
                let total = (try? await model.tokenCount(for: session.transcript)) ?? -1
                print("\(label): ERROR \(error) transcript=\(total)")
            }
        }
        func runInsight(_ label: String, prompt: String) async {
            let session = LanguageModelSession(tools: insightTools, instructions: insightInstructions)
            do {
                let r = try await session.respond(to: prompt, generating: GeneratedInsight.self)
                let total = try await model.tokenCount(for: session.transcript)
                print("\(label): transcript=\(total) headline=\"\(r.content.headline)\" entries=\(session.transcript.count)")
            } catch {
                let total = (try? await model.tokenCount(for: session.transcript)) ?? -1
                print("\(label): ERROR \(error) transcript=\(total)")
            }
        }

        await runInsight("insight", prompt: insightPromptPlain)
        await runInsight("insight+disagreement", prompt: insightPromptDisagree)
        await run("Q status", instructions: instructions(appIntent: true), tools: qaTools,
                  prompt: "How much caffeine will I have at bedtime?")
        await run("Q log", instructions: instructions(appIntent: true), tools: qaTools,
                  prompt: "I just had a cold brew")
        drinksPerDay = 6
        await run("Q week (6/day)", instructions: instructions(appIntent: true), tools: qaTools,
                  prompt: "What did I drink each day this week? Go through every day from today back to 6 days ago.")
        drinksPerDay = 12
        await run("Q week (12/day)", instructions: instructions(appIntent: true), tools: qaTools,
                  prompt: "What did I drink each day this week? Go through every day from today back to 6 days ago.")
        await run("Q everything", instructions: instructions(appIntent: true), tools: qaTools,
                  prompt: "Tell me my caffeine now, my cutoff, my level at 11pm, and what I drank today and yesterday.")
    }
}

```

**15:32:06 · Tool result**

````
## Rules

1. **Scope: the entire project.** Log every interaction with an AI tool about Half-Life, whether or not it changes a file. That includes:
   - generating code, tests, documentation, or configuration;
   - code review and debugging;
   - planning and design discussions;
   - questions and explanations.

   This applies to every contributor and every AI tool: coding agents, chat assistants, and IDE assistants.
2. **One entry per task.** A task is one request to an AI tool plus the follow-up interaction needed to complete it. A session with several unrelated requests gets several entries. Inline code-completion tools (e.g. Xcode predictive code completion) are logged once per working session in which they were used, not once per suggestion.
3. **Timestamps.**
   1. Every entry records when its task started and ended.
   2. Its **Interactions** timeline gives the time of each human prompt or decision.
   3. Timestamps are local time with UTC offset in the form `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM ±hhmm`, exactly what `date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M %z'` prints. Within the Interactions timeline, `HH:MM` is enough while the date is unchanged.
   4. Take timestamps from the clock at the time, never from memory afterwards. If a time has to be reconstructed, prefer the session transcript's timestamps (Rule 9). Otherwise, prefix the time with `~` and say what it was reconstructed from.
4. **Chronological and append-only.** Append entries at the bottom of the Log section, in order of start time. An entry may be revised while its task's changes are uncommitted. Once committed, it is never edited or deleted. To correct a committed entry, add a new entry that references it.
5. **When to log.** Write the entry before the task is reported as finished. If the task changed files, commit the entry together with those changes. Otherwise commit it on its own.
6. **Honesty.** Record what the AI actually did, including incorrect answers, rejected suggestions, withdrawn work, and outputs that needed significant human correction.
7. **No sensitive data.** Never paste secrets, credentials, personal data, health data, or customer data into an entry or a transcript (Rule 9). Summarize prompts instead of copying them verbatim when they contain such data.
8. **Accountability.** Every entry names the human responsible for the interaction and for reviewing and accepting its outcome.
9. **Transcripts.** The full transcript of every AI session is kept in `ai_transcripts/`, one file per session.
   1. Claude Code sessions are exported with `/usr/bin/python3 scripts/export_transcripts.py`, which renders Claude Code's local session files as Markdown. Re-run it before every commit so each committed transcript covers its session up to that point. Transcripts are generated files: never edit one by hand. A committed transcript changes only by being re-exported.
   2. A transcript includes every human prompt, reply, and tool call in full, each with its time. Tool results are cut to their first 40 lines. Images, system context, and harness metadata are left out. Claude Code doesn't store the AI's reasoning or the progress updates it writes between tool calls, so transcripts can't include them.
   3. The exporter redacts email addresses and the home directory path. It can't recognize secrets or health data typed into a prompt or read from a file, so review the transcript diff before committing, and apply Rule 7 to it.
   4. For other AI tools, save the tool's own export of the conversation in `ai_transcripts/` if it offers one. Otherwise, say in the entry's **Transcript** field that no transcript exists.
   5. Every entry names the transcript of the session it happened in. One session can cover several entries, and an entry whose task spans sessions lists each transcript.

### Entry format

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM ±hhmm — <short title>

- **Started:** YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM ±hhmm
- **Ended:** YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM ±hhmm
- **Human:** <name of the responsible person>
- **AI tool / model:** <e.g. Claude Code / Claude Opus 5>
- **Transcript:** <path(s) under `ai_transcripts/`, or "None" and why>
- **Type:** <one or more of: change, question, review, debugging, planning>
- **Request:** <one or two sentences summarizing what was asked>
- **Interactions:**
````

        print("\n== Synthetic full transcripts (framework formatting included) ==")
        func text(_ s: String) -> [Transcript.Segment] { [.text(Transcript.TextSegment(content: s))] }
        func call(_ name: String, _ json: String) throws -> Transcript.ToolCall {
            Transcript.ToolCall(id: UUID().uuidString, toolName: name, arguments: try GeneratedContent(json: json))
        }
        // A three-sentence answer, the most the instructions allow, about 70 words.
        let answer = """
            You have about 85 mg of caffeine in your body now, and it will be down to about 34 mg at your 10:30 PM \
            bedtime. The latest time for your usual latte, 2 shots, is 2:30 PM, and at 11:00 PM you'll have about 31 mg. \
            Today you logged 812 mg across six drinks, and yesterday you logged 812 mg as well.
            """
        func transcript(
            instructions: String, tools: [any Tool], prompt: String, format: Transcript.ResponseFormat? = nil,
            calls: [(String, String, String)], answer: String
        ) throws -> [Transcript.Entry] {
            var entries: [Transcript.Entry] = [
                .instructions(Transcript.Instructions(
                    segments: text(instructions), toolDefinitions: tools.map { Transcript.ToolDefinition(tool: $0) })),
                .prompt(Transcript.Prompt(segments: text(prompt), responseFormat: format)),
            ]
            if !calls.isEmpty {
                let toolCalls = try calls.map { try call($0.0, $0.1) }
                entries.append(.toolCalls(Transcript.ToolCalls(toolCalls)))
                for (tc, c) in zip(toolCalls, calls) {
                    entries.append(.toolOutput(Transcript.ToolOutput(id: tc.id, toolName: c.0, segments: text(c.2))))
                }
            }
            entries.append(.response(Transcript.Response(assetIDs: [], segments: text(answer))))
            return entries
        }
        func count(_ label: String, _ entries: [Transcript.Entry]) async throws -> Int {
            let n = try await model.tokenCount(for: entries)
            print("\(label): \(n) / \(model.contextSize)  (\(n * 100 / model.contextSize)%)")
            return n
        }

import ComposableArchitecture
import Foundation
import OSLog

@Reducer nonisolated struct WhatWeNoticedFeature {
    private static let logger = Logger(for: WhatWeNoticedFeature.self)

    /// What the card shows.
    @ObservableState
    struct State: Equatable {
        /// What the card shows, or `nil` while it's hidden.
        var card: InsightCard?
        /// The request the insight is written for, or `nil` before the first card.
        var request: InsightRequest?
        /// The finding in the model's words, or `nil` until it's written.
        var insight: Insight?
    }

    /// What can happen to the card.
    enum Action {
        /// Subscribes to the card, for as long as the tab is on screen.
        case task
        /// The card changed, or is hidden.
        case cardUpdated(InsightCard?)
        /// The model wrote the insight for a request.
        case insightWritten(InsightRequest, Insight)
        /// The user answered "Feel right?".
        case answered(InsightFeedback.Answer)
    }

    private enum CancelID {
        case write
    }

    @Dependency(\.observeInsightCard) private var observeInsightCard
    @Dependency(\.writeInsight) private var writeInsight

49:| The resting heart rate screen (12:03, in half-life-28's session) | The next day's resting heart rate after caffeine nights, against the other days, over 30 days. A headline, the two groups, and a 30-day dot chart. A finding only with 5 days in each group, and a pattern only beyond twice the standard error. A use case that combines two repositories' streams and executes the rule. |
56:The first card is one finding about the user's own nights, which the rules work out and Apple Intelligence's on-device model puts into words. It's optional: it's hidden while the model is unavailable, including in a language the model doesn't support (<doc:LanguageModel>), without enough nights, and after the user says it doesn't feel right. **Status: built** on 2026-09-13. Its audit with the card showing fails for a known reason (see "Known issues"). The owner decided its design on 2026-09-13, from 11:16 to 12:1x.
68:- **The confidence** comes from the smaller group: fewer than 5 nights is too few, and the card is hidden; 5 to 9 is **"an early sign"**; 10 or more is **"consistent so far"**.
109:- **A number check** discards any answer that holds a number the tool didn't give, and the card stays hidden. So the tool gives the total nights too, which the owner added on 2026-09-13, and the model copies the sum rather than calculating it.
129:4. **The card:** ``ObserveInsightCardUseCase``, ``WhatWeNoticedFeature``, and ``WhatWeNoticedView`` are built. ``InsightsView`` shows the card first, and starts its task, because a hidden view can't start its own. Its UI tests use the simulated model under a launch key (<doc:LanguageModel>), with the demo's nights.
231:- **Each button** shows the kind's SF Symbol, the Today card's, in its data color on a light tile (`bed.double.fill` in `dataSleep`, `figure.walk` in `dataActivity`, `heart.fill` in `dataHeart`), then its name and a chevron. The name says what the button is, so color is never the only signal (constitution Article VI.3), and the symbols are hidden from VoiceOver.
246:- **``HealthDataListFeature``** holds the available kinds. `task` subscribes, each set becomes a `kindsUpdated` action, and `kindTapped` is the button a user tapped. `kinds` is the buttons, in order, and `isShown` hides the card with none. ``InsightsView`` starts its `task`, because a hidden card can't start its own.
288:4. The candidate whose line leaves the least squared error is the tolerance. On a tie, the lower one wins.
336:The tolerance is the only thing the screen stores. It's derived from sleep, so it stays on the device, with complete file protection, left out of iCloud backups, and never synced (constitution Articles V.3.4 and V.4). No value, count, night, or tolerance is logged (Article XI.6). Storing the tolerance logs a fixed message at `debug`, and a failure to read or store logs its error's domain and code at `error` (Article XI.7).
356:| No clear difference | The difference is within twice its standard error |
357:| About 1,500 fewer (or more) steps after a caffeine night | The difference is beyond twice its standard error |
359:- **The standard error** comes from each side's own spread, so a user whose steps swing widely needs a bigger difference before the screen names one.
381:| When a difference is named (12:03) | Beyond twice its standard error, with at least 5 days on each side |
424:| A difference more than twice its standard error, and at least 1 bpm | The difference, such as "+3 bpm", and whether it was higher or lower |
427:The standard error is Welch's: each group's sample variance over its size, summed, then square-rooted. The 1 bpm floor is the AI's addition, still to be confirmed. The screen shows whole beats per minute, so a smaller difference would read "+0 bpm". The rule never assumes a direction. A lower heart rate after caffeine nights is reported the same way.
429:The demo script raises the next day's resting heart rate by 3 bpm after a late cup, so the demo data shows a pattern. A simulation of the script, with the standard half-life, found the days after a caffeine night 2.5 to 3.1 bpm higher at thresholds from 40 to 60 mg, each more than twice its standard error. It's a simulation of the script, not a reading of the app.
461:- **A failure to read the window's inputs is logged at `error`,** with the error's domain and code only.
572:| NUMCHECK-2 | A number the facts don't hold fails the finding. |
588:| FEEDUSE-2 | ``RecordInsightFeedbackUseCase`` stores the answer, with the finding it answered, at the time ``CurrentTimeRepository`` gives, and passes on the repository's error. Features don't read the clock. |
591:| CARD-2 | While the model is unavailable, it sends `nil`, and the card is hidden. |
600:| NOTICED-4 | A hidden card is hidden. |
601:| NOTICED-5 | When the insight can't be written, or fails the number check, the card has no insight, and stays hidden. |
603:| NOTICED-7 | An answer is recorded through ``RecordInsightFeedbackUseCase``, with the card's confidence and the insight. Without an insight there's nothing to answer, and a failure to store it is logged. |
680:| STEPSRULE-5 | A difference within twice its standard error is no clear difference. |
681:| STEPSRULE-6 | A difference beyond twice its standard error is that many fewer, or more, steps after a caffeine night. |
719:| RHRCOMP-4 | A difference more than twice its standard error, and at least 1 bpm, is a pattern, higher or lower. |
720:| RHRCOMP-5 | A difference within twice its standard error isn't clear. |
737:- **The Insights tab's audit fails after scrolling to the end** (UI-INS-3, UI-INS-8, and UI-INS-17). ```
import Foundation

struct ObserveInsightCardUseCase: UseCase {
    /// The repository that owns the Sleep screen's analysis.
    let sleepTolerance: any SleepToleranceRepository
    /// The repository that stores the "Feel right?" answers.
    let insightFeedback: any InsightFeedbackRepository
    /// The repository that owns the language model's availability.
    let languageModel: any LanguageModelRepository

    /// A value from one of the three streams.
    private enum Update: Sendable {
        case analysis(SleepCaffeineAnalysis)
        case answers([InsightFeedback])
        case availability(LanguageModelAvailability)
    }

    /// Returns a stream of the card: the first once all three repositories have sent a value, then each change. It
    /// finishes when all three repositories' streams have.
    ///
    /// - Parameter input: None.
    func execute(_ input: Void) -> AsyncStream<InsightCard?> {
        let updates = Self.merge(sleepTolerance.analysis(), insightFeedback.feedback(), languageModel.availability())
        return AsyncStream { continuation in
            let task = Task {
                var analysis: SleepCaffeineAnalysis?
                var answers: [InsightFeedback]?
                var availability: LanguageModelAvailability?
                var lastSent: InsightCard??
                for await update in updates {
                    switch update {
                    case let .analysis(value): analysis = value
                    case let .answers(value): answers = value
                    case let .availability(value): availability = value
                    }
                    guard let analysis, let answers, let availability else { continue }
                    let card = Self.card(from: analysis, answers: answers, availability: availability)
                    if let lastSent, lastSent == card { continue }
                    lastSent = .some(card)
                    continuation.yield(card)
```

### 15:36:39 · Claude

The Insights card's prompt is nowhere near the limit. One full insight session measures **769 of 4,096 tokens (18%)**, including the tool call and the answer. If the error really is the context window, the model must be looping inside the session until it fills up.

I counted with Apple's own tokenizer in an iOS 26.5 simulator, using exact copies of our instructions, tool definitions and prompts (this Mac's macOS beta lacks the counting API). The tool outputs were sample text at realistic sizes, not read from the app. Apple Intelligence is off there, so I couldn't run the real model or reproduce your error.

## Insights card 1

| Scenario | Tokens | Share of 4,096 |
|---|---|---|
| Normal insight | 695 | 17% |
| After a "Not really" (the earlier headline and sentence go back in) | 769 | 18% |
| `compareCaffeineTiming` called 10 times | 2,011 | 49% |
| `compareCaffeineTiming` called 25 times | 4,081 | 99% |
| Sentence running on to about 1,600 words | 3,936 | 96% |
| Facts in the prompt, no tool | 655 | 15% |

Only two things can fill the window: the model calling `compareCaffeineTiming` over and over, or a headline or sentence that never ends. The 8- and 25-word limits are only descriptions, and nothing in the app caps the output length (no `maximumResponseTokens`). The card also hides itself on any error, so you only see the failure in the log.

**To confirm the cause:** find the `Couldn't write an insight:` line in the device's log. With domain `FoundationModels.LanguageModelSession.GenerationError`, code **0** means the context window overflowed. Code 2 would mean the safety guardrails blocked it, which is a different fix. Those code numbers follow Swift's default error mapping, which I didn't confirm on a device.

## Asking Half-Life a question

A question starts at **931 tokens (22%)** before any tool runs. The tool definitions take 697 of those, and `logDrink` alone takes 251 because it lists all 13 drinks.

| Scenario | Share of 4,096 |
|---|---|
| Status question, or logging a drink | 24% |
| Five tools at once | 37% |
| Last 7 days at 6 drinks a day | 55% |
| Last 7 days at 12 drinks a day | 81% |
| Last 14 days at 6 drinks a day | 88% |
| **Last 30 days at 4 drinks a day** | **128%, overflows** |
| **A typed question of about 13,800 characters** | **fills the window** |

- `getDrinksOnDay` accepts up to 90 days ago, and nothing limits how many times the model calls it. "What did I drink this month?" overflows.
- The question has no length limit. Siri dictation is short, but a Shortcut can pass in any amount of text.
- A context-window error isn't handled on its own, so the user just sees "couldn't answer."
- The six tools the Language Model article lists as still to come would add about 100–180 tokens each to every question.

## Proposed fixes (not made yet)

1. **The card:** put the facts straight into the insight's prompt and drop the tool. There's then nothing to loop on, it's cheaper, and it's one fewer model round trip. The facts still come from the card's request, so the number check still works. This reverses a recorded decision (LMSRC-6), so it's your call.
2. **The card:** cap the answer's length (`maximumResponseTokens`, about 150), so a runaway sentence fails fast instead of filling the window.
3. **Logging:** log which kind of error it was, not just the domain and code. The kind contains no health data.
4. **Questions:** give the tools a shared limit on calls per question, cap how many drinks `getDrinksOnDay` lists, limit the question's length, and reply with a clear message when the context window is exceeded.
5. **A budget test:** the tokenizer works in the simulator without Apple Intelligence, so a Swift Testing test can fail the build whenever instructions plus tools pass a set budget.

Which of these do you want built? I'd start with 1 to 3 for the card. The first reverses the recorded decision, so it needs your OK.

I appended this task's entry to `ai_log.md`; nothing is committed. I also told half-life-ce that this session doesn't touch its date-of-birth files.

import Foundation

struct SimulatedLanguageModelDataSource: LanguageModelDataSource {
    /// Whether the UI test asked for the model.
    let isAvailable: Bool
    /// The calendar the tool's facts are formatted in.
    let calendar: Calendar

    /// Whether the UI test asked for the model (SIMLM-1).
    func availability() -> LanguageModelAvailability {
        isAvailable ? .available : .unavailable
    }

    /// Answers with the same sentence, whatever the instruction (SIMLM-3).
    func respond(to instruction: LanguageModelInstruction) async throws -> String {
        "This is the simulated language model's answer."
    }

    /// Writes an insight whose sentence is the facts' line about time asleep (SIMLM-2).
    func writeInsight(_ request: InsightRequest) async throws -> WrittenInsight {
        let facts = CaffeineTimingTool(pattern: request.pattern, format: LanguageModelFormat(calendar: calendar)).facts
        let timeAsleep = facts.split(separator: "\n").first { $0.hasPrefix("Time asleep:") }.map(String.init) ?? facts
        let insight = Insight(headline: "A pattern in your nights", sentence: timeAsleep)
        return WrittenInsight(insight: insight, facts: facts)
    }
}

protocol LanguageModelDataSource: Sendable {
    /// Reads whether the model can be used now.
    ///
    /// The framework doesn't announce changes, so the repository calls this again at each whole minute.
    func availability() -> LanguageModelAvailability

    /// Answers one instruction in a new session, with the tools its origin allows, then discards the session.
    ///
    /// - Parameter instruction: The prompt, and where it came from.
    /// - Returns: The model's response.
    /// - Throws: The model's error if it couldn't respond.
    func respond(to instruction: LanguageModelInstruction) async throws -> String

```

**15:44:57 · Tool result**

````
The first card is one finding about the user's own nights, which the rules work out and Apple Intelligence's on-device model puts into words. It's optional: it's hidden while the model is unavailable, including in a language the model doesn't support (<doc:LanguageModel>), without enough nights, and after the user says it doesn't feel right. **Status: built** on 2026-09-13. Its audit with the card showing fails for a known reason (see "Known issues"). The owner decided its design on 2026-09-13, from 11:16 to 12:1x.

### The finding

Over the last 30 days, it compares the nights the user fell asleep with more than the sleep threshold in their body, against the nights with the threshold or less, by **time asleep**.

The Sleep screen's analysis finds the nights and compares them (``SleepToleranceRule``), and card 1 turns its comparison into the finding (``SleepPatternRule``):

- **A night** is a stretch of sleep of at least 3 hours, grouped as ``SleepNightRule`` groups them, from any tracker, with or without stages. Time in bed alone isn't sleep, so it doesn't count. A night counts if it ended in the 30 days, and began once drinks had been logged for 2 days.
- **Its caffeine** is ``CaffeineDecayRule``'s level at the moment Health says the user **fell asleep**: the start of the night's first stretch of sleep. It uses the drinks of the week before, and the curve's half-life and absorption.
- **The threshold** is the user's learned tolerance, or 40 mg without one.
- **The direction** compares the two groups' average time asleep: **shorter** or **longer** on the nights over the threshold, or **about the same** when the averages are less than **15 minutes** apart.
- **The confidence** comes from the smaller group: fewer than 5 nights is too few, and the card is hidden; 5 to 9 is **"an early sign"**; 10 or more is **"consistent so far"**.

The finding never says caffeine caused anything, never says how much sleep was lost, and never gives advice. It gives the model the direction only, not the averages.

### The model's prompts

The owner reviewed and approved these on 2026-09-13.

- **The session's instructions** are the standing rules every session gets (<doc:LanguageModel>), including "Answer in" the user's language, named from the locale, plus these:

  ```text
  You're writing the one finding on the user's Insights card, from the compareCaffeineTiming tool.
  Write a headline of at most 8 words, and one sentence of at most 25 words.
  Say what the tool found about the user's nights, and how many nights it's based on.
  Use the tool's confidence words exactly: "an early sign" or "consistent so far".
  Describe a pattern in the user's nights. Never say caffeine caused, cost, or took away sleep, and never say how much sleep the user lost.
  Don't give advice, and don't tell the user what to do.
  If the user disagreed with an earlier finding, take a different approach from that one.
  ```

- **Its only tool** is `compareCaffeineTiming`, which reports the finding with every number already formatted:

  ```text
  Period: the last 30 days, Aug 14 to Sep 12.
  Caffeine when you fell asleep: over 40 mg on 6 nights, 40 mg or under on 17 nights.
  Nights counted: 23.
  Time asleep: shorter on the nights over 40 mg than on the others.
  Confidence: an early sign.
````

import Foundation

/// The check that the model's finding holds no number the facts don't: a guard on the Insights tab's first card.
///
/// The rules calculate, and the model only phrases (see the Language Model article). So every number in the model's
/// headline and sentence has to be one the `compareCaffeineTiming` tool gave it. A finding with any other number is
/// discarded, and the card stays hidden. Numbers are runs of decimal digits in any script, so "٦" and "6" are the same.
/// Numbers written as words aren't caught. It holds no state. See the Insights article, NUMCHECK-1 to NUMCHECK-4.
struct InsightNumberRule {
    /// Returns whether every number in `text` is also in `facts`.

/// in the Language Model article, with a fake data source.

```
// Sizes the insight answer, to choose maximumResponseTokens, and the new tool-free insight transcript.
import Foundation
import FoundationModels

@Generable(description: "The one finding on the user's Insights card.")
struct GeneratedInsight {
    @Guide(description: "A headline of at most 8 words.")
    let headline: String
    @Guide(description: "One sentence of at most 25 words.")
    let sentence: String
}

let instructions = [
    "You write short answers for Half-Life, an app that shows how much caffeine is in the user's body and when "
        + "it wears off.",
    "Get every number from the facts. Never calculate, estimate, or invent one.",
    "Repeat amounts and days exactly as the facts give them.",
    "If the facts say something isn't available, say so plainly.",
    "Don't diagnose, and don't give medical advice.",
    "Answer in three sentences or fewer.",
    "Answer in English.",
    "You're writing the one finding on the user's Insights card, from the facts in the prompt.",
    "Write a headline of at most 8 words, and one sentence of at most 25 words.",
    "Say what the facts show about the user's nights, and how many nights it's based on.",
    "Use the facts' confidence words exactly: \"an early sign\" or \"consistent so far\".",
    "Describe a pattern in the user's nights. Never say caffeine caused, cost, or took away sleep, and never say "
        + "how much sleep the user lost.",
    "Don't give advice, and don't tell the user what to do.",
    "If the user disagreed with an earlier finding, take a different approach from that one.",
].joined(separator: "\n")

let facts = """
    Period: the last 30 days, Aug 14 to Sep 12.
    Caffeine when you fell asleep: over 50 mg on 11 nights, 50 mg or under on 16 nights.
    Nights counted: 27.
    Time asleep: shorter on the nights over 50 mg than on the others.
    Confidence: consistent so far.
    3 nights aren't counted: no sleep was recorded, or no drinks were logged before them yet.
    """

let prompt = """
    Write today's finding for the user's Insights card.

    The facts:
    \(facts)

    The user disagreed with this earlier finding:
    Headline: "Late caffeine nights run a little shorter"
    Sentence: "On the 11 nights you fell asleep over 50 mg, your time asleep was shorter than on the other 16, which is consistent so far."
    Take a different approach from the one in that finding.
    """

let answers: [(String, String)] = [
    ("en, 7 + 25 words", #"{"headline":"Late caffeine nights run a little shorter","sentence":"On the 11 nights you fell asleep over 50 mg, your time asleep was shorter than on the other 16, which is consistent so far."}"#),
    ("fr", #"{"headline":"Les nuits caféinées tardives sont un peu plus courtes","sentence":"Sur les 11 nuits où vous vous êtes endormi avec plus de 50 mg, votre temps de sommeil était plus court que sur les 16 autres, ce qui reste constant jusqu'ici."}"#),
    ("de", #"{"headline":"Nächte mit spätem Koffein fallen etwas kürzer aus","sentence":"In den 11 Nächten, in denen Sie mit mehr als 50 mg eingeschlafen sind, war Ihre Schlafzeit kürzer als in den anderen 16, was bisher durchgehend so ist."}"#),
    ("ja", #"{"headline":"カフェインが多い夜は睡眠が少し短め","sentence":"50 mgを超えて眠りについた11夜は、ほかの16夜より睡眠時間が短く、これまでのところ一貫しています。"}"#),
    ("pt-BR", #"{"headline":"Noites com cafeína tardia são um pouco mais curtas","sentence":"Nas 11 noites em que você adormeceu com mais de 50 mg, seu tempo de sono foi menor do que nas outras 16, o que se mantém consistente até agora."}"#),
]

@main
struct Measure {
    static func main() async throws {
        let model = SystemLanguageModel.default
        for (label, answer) in answers {
            print("answer \(label):", try await model.tokenCount(for: answer))
        }
        let entries: [Transcript.Entry] = [
            .instructions(Transcript.Instructions(
                segments: [.text(Transcript.TextSegment(content: instructions))], toolDefinitions: [])),
            .prompt(Transcript.Prompt(
                segments: [.text(Transcript.TextSegment(content: prompt))],
                responseFormat: Transcript.ResponseFormat(schema: GeneratedInsight.generationSchema))),
            .response(Transcript.Response(
                assetIDs: [], segments: [.text(Transcript.TextSegment(content: answers[1].1))])),
        ]
        let total = try await model.tokenCount(for: entries)
        print("new insight transcript (with disagreement, fr answer): \(total) / \(model.contextSize)")
    }
}

```

    @Test func onlyAnAppIntentsInstructionsMentionLoggingADrink() {

    // MARK: - LMSRC-8: a failure is logged with the kind of error it was

    struct OtherError: Error {}

    static let context = LanguageModelSession.GenerationError.Context(debugDescription: "")

    /// Every error the model can generate, with the kind the log names it by.
    static let generationErrors: [(LanguageModelSession.GenerationError, String)] = [
        (.exceededContextWindowSize(context), "exceededContextWindowSize"),
        (.assetsUnavailable(context), "assetsUnavailable"),
        (.guardrailViolation(context), "guardrailViolation"),
        (.unsupportedGuide(context), "unsupportedGuide"),
        (.unsupportedLanguageOrLocale(context), "unsupportedLanguageOrLocale"),
        (.decodingFailure(context), "decodingFailure"),
        (.rateLimited(context), "rateLimited"),
        (.concurrentRequests(context), "concurrentRequests"),
        (.refusal(.init(transcriptEntries: []), context), "refusal"),
    ]

    @Test(arguments: generationErrors)
    func aGenerationErrorIsNamedByItsCase(error: LanguageModelSession.GenerationError, kind: String) {
        #expect(FoundationModelLanguageModelDataSource.failureKind(of: error) == kind)
    }

    @Test func aToolsErrorIsNamedAsOne() throws {
        let tool = CaffeineStatusTool(
            caffeineDecay: FakeCaffeineDecayRepository(),
            format: LanguageModelFormat(calendar: try LanguageModelFormatTests.calendar("America/New_York")))
        let error = LanguageModelSession.ToolCallError(tool: tool, underlyingError: OtherError())

        #expect(FoundationModelLanguageModelDataSource.failureKind(of: error) == "toolCallError")
    }

    @Test func anyOtherErrorIsNamedOther() {
        #expect(FoundationModelLanguageModelDataSource.failureKind(of: OtherError()) == "other")
    }
}

| An insight's session has no tools. Its facts are in the prompt, and its answer is capped at 150 tokens. The owner decided this on 2026-09-13, at 15:43, replacing the `compareCaffeineTiming` tool. | A context audit measured a normal insight at 18% of the model's 4,096-token window, so only a loop inside the session could overflow it: the model calling `compareCaffeineTiming` again and again (about 25 calls fill the window), or a sentence that never ends. With no tool, there's nothing to call again, and the cap stops a runaway answer. The facts still come from the request, so the model's words still can't disagree with the card. |

  ─▶ InsightNumberRule: the insight is kept only if every number in it is in the tool's facts
```

- **Guided generation.** The model answers into `GeneratedInsight`, a `@Generable` mirror of ``Insight``, whose guides ask for a headline of at most 8 words and one sentence of at most 25.
- **The number check.** The data source returns the insight with the facts its tool reports, and the repository executes ``InsightNumberRule``. An insight with any other number throws ``LanguageModelError/ungrounded``, and the card stays hidden. Numbers written as words aren't caught.
- **The finding comes with the request.** ``InsightRequest`` carries the ``SleepPattern`` the card holds, and `compareCaffeineTiming` reports it, so the model's words can't disagree with the card.
````
- `new_string`:

````
  ─▶ LanguageModelDataSource.writeInsight(_:)
       ─▶ a new LanguageModelSession(instructions:), with no tools
            ─▶ a prompt with the request's facts
            ─▶ one GeneratedInsight, through guided generation, at most 150 tokens, then discarded
  ─▶ InsightNumberRule: the insight is kept only if every number in it is in the facts
```

- **The facts are in the prompt.** ``InsightRequest`` carries the ``SleepPattern`` the card holds, and `InsightFacts` writes it as a few lines of text, with every number already formatted. The prompt gives the model those lines, so its words can't disagree with the card. The session has no tools, so the model has nothing to call again and again.
- **Guided generation.** The model answers into `GeneratedInsight`, a `@Generable` mirror of ``Insight``, whose guides ask for a headline of at most 8 words and one sentence of at most 25. The guides are only descriptions, so the answer is also capped at **150 tokens**, twice the longest valid answer measured (74 tokens, in French). A runaway answer fails at the cap instead of filling the context window, and the card stays hidden.
- **The number check.** The data source returns the insight with the facts it gave the model, and the repository executes ``InsightNumberRule``. An insight with any other number throws ``LanguageModelError/ungrounded``, and the card stays hidden. Numbers written as words aren't caught.
````

- `new_string`:

```
- **The context window is small.** `SystemLanguageModel.contextSize` is 4,096 tokens, for everything in the session: instructions, tool definitions, the prompt, tool calls and their output, and the answer. Measure new tools with `tokenCount(for:)` as they're added. It runs in the simulator without Apple Intelligence. An audit on 2026-09-13 measured, with the framework's own formatting:

  | Session | Tokens | Of 4,096 |
  |---------|--------|----------|
  | An insight, with a disagreement and its answer | 665 | 16% |
  | A question, before any tool call. The five tools' definitions are 697 of it, and `logDrink`'s alone 251. | 931 | 22% |
  | A question that reads 7 days, at 12 drinks a day | 3,331 | 81% |
  | A question that reads 30 days, at 4 drinks a day | 5,273 | 128% |

  A question's tool calls and the question's length aren't limited yet, so a question about a long stretch of days can overflow the window. The audit's other proposals, a call budget, a cap on `getDrinksOnDay`'s lines, and a limit on the question's length, are still to decide.
```

- `new_string`:

```
- **Nothing the model reads or writes is logged.** Prompts, responses, tool arguments, and tool output all carry health data (Article XI.6), so they're never logged, at any level. The data source logs a failed response or insight at `error`, with the kind of error, its domain, and its code, all `.public`. The kind is the case of the framework's `GenerationError`, such as `exceededContextWindowSize` or `guardrailViolation`, or `toolCallError` when a tool threw, or `other`. The error's description, and the context the framework attaches to it, aren't logged, because they can hold the prompt. The repository logs an instruction it refuses because the model is unavailable at `notice`, without its text, and an insight it discards for its numbers the same way.
- **Only a finding from Health data reaches the model.** An insight's facts give it counts of nights, a direction, and a confidence, derived from sleep, and never an amount of sleep. They stay on the device, with the model.
```

//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |
// |  | |  ||  |_|  ||   | |   |    |   |    |       ||       ||  | |  |
// |  |_|  ||       ||   | |   |___ |   |___ |       ||  _    ||  |_|  |
// |      | |       ||   | |       ||       ||   _   || | |   ||      |
// |____||_||_______||___| |_______||_______||__| |__||_|  |__||____||_|
//
// Half-Life InsightFacts
//

import Foundation

/// The facts of the Insights tab's first finding, with every number already formatted, for the insight's prompt and
/// the number check.
///
/// It writes the ``SleepPattern`` that came with the request, which is the one the card holds, so the model's words
/// can't disagree with the card. It gives the direction only, never the averages, so the model can't say how much sleep
/// was lost. It gives the total nights too, so the model copies the sum rather than calculating it, which the number
/// check would reject (LMFACT-1, LMFACT-2).
struct InsightFacts: Sendable {
    /// The finding the facts describe.
    let pattern: SleepPattern
    /// Formats the counts, amounts, and days, in the calendar the finding was made in.
    let format: LanguageModelFormat

    /// The finding, as the lines the prompt gives the model. The number check compares the model's answer with them.
    var text: String {
        let threshold = format.milligrams(pattern.threshold.milligrams)
        let firstDay = format.shortDay(pattern.period.start)
        let lastDay = format.shortDay(pattern.period.end.addingTimeInterval(-1))
        let over = nights(pattern.nightsOver)
        let under = nights(pattern.nightsUnder)
        var lines = [
            "Period: the last \(format.count(pattern.days)) days, \(firstDay) to \(lastDay).",
            "Caffeine when you fell asleep: over \(threshold) on \(over), \(threshold) or under on \(under).",
            "Nights counted: \(format.count(pattern.nightsOver + pattern.nightsUnder)).",
            "Time asleep: \(direction(over: threshold)).",
            "Confidence: \(confidence).",
        ]
        let uncounted = pattern.uncountedNights
        let reason = "no sleep was recorded, or no drinks were logged before"
        if uncounted == 1 {
            lines.append("\(format.count(uncounted)) night isn't counted: \(reason) it yet.")
        } else if uncounted > 1 {
            lines.append("\(format.count(uncounted)) nights aren't counted: \(reason) them yet.")
        }
        return lines.joined(separator: "\n")
    }

    private func nights(_ count: Int) -> String {
        "\(format.count(count)) \(count == 1 ? "night" : "nights")"
    }

    private func direction(over threshold: String) -> String {
        switch pattern.direction {
        case .shorter: "shorter on the nights over \(threshold) than on the others"
        case .longer: "longer on the nights over \(threshold) than on the others"
        case .aboutTheSame: "about the same on the nights over \(threshold) as on the others"
        }
    }

    private var confidence: String {
        switch pattern.confidence {
        case .tooFew: "too few nights to say anything yet"
        case .earlySign: "an early sign"
        case .consistent: "consistent so far"
        }
    }
}

```

    static func text(for origin: LanguageModelInstruction.Origin, locale: Locale) -> String {
        var rules = [
            "You write short answers for Half-Life, an app that shows how much caffeine is in the user's body and when "
                + "it wears off.",
            "Get every number, time, and drink from a tool. Never calculate, estimate, or invent one.",
            "Repeat amounts and times exactly as the tools give them.",
            "If a tool says something isn't available, say so plainly.",
            "Don't diagnose, and don't give medical advice.",
            "Answer in three sentences or fewer.",
            "Answer in \(languageName(for: locale)).",
        ]
        if origin == .appIntent {
            rules.append("Log a drink only when the user says they drank it.")
        }
        return rules.joined(separator: "\n")
    }

    /// The instructions for a session that writes the Insights tab's first finding: the standing rules for text the
    /// app writes, then the insight rules (LMSRC-4).
    ///
    /// - Parameter locale: The user's locale, whose language the model answers in.
    static func insightText(locale: Locale) -> String {
        ([text(for: .app, locale: locale)] + insightRules).joined(separator: "\n")
    }

    /// The prompt for the Insights tab's first finding (LMSRC-5).
    ///
    /// After a "Not really", it adds the finding the user disagreed with, and asks for a different approach from it,
    /// so the model learns what kind of finding the user doesn't like.
    ///
    /// - Parameter disagreement: The finding the user last answered "Not really" to, or `nil` if there's none.
    static func insightPrompt(disagreement: InsightFeedback?) -> String {
        let prompt = "Write today's finding for the user's Insights card."
        guard let disagreement else { return prompt }

    static func text(for origin: LanguageModelInstruction.Origin, locale: Locale) -> String {
        var rules = standingRules(from: .tools, locale: locale)
        if origin == .appIntent {
            rules.append("Log a drink only when the user says they drank it.")
        }
        return rules.joined(separator: "\n")
    }

    /// The instructions for a session that writes the Insights tab's first finding: the standing rules, taking every
    /// number from the facts in the prompt, because the session has no tools, then the insight rules (LMSRC-4).
    ///
    /// - Parameter locale: The user's locale, whose language the model answers in.
    static func insightText(locale: Locale) -> String {
        (standingRules(from: .facts, locale: locale) + insightRules).joined(separator: "\n")
    }

    /// The prompt for the Insights tab's first finding, with its facts (LMSRC-5).
    ///
    /// After a "Not really", it adds the finding the user disagreed with, and asks for a different approach from it,
    /// so the model learns what kind of finding the user doesn't like.
    ///
    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - facts: The finding's facts, from ``InsightFacts``.
    ///   - disagreement: The finding the user last answered "Not really" to, or `nil` if there's none.
    static func insightPrompt(facts: String, disagreement: InsightFeedback?) -> String {
        let prompt = """
            Write today's finding for the user's Insights card.

            The facts:
            \(facts)
            """
        guard let disagreement else { return prompt }

            "Answer in \(languageName(for: locale)).",

    /// Builds the one tool for a session that writes the Insights tab's first finding: `compareCaffeineTiming`, which
    /// reports the request's finding (LMSRC-6).
    ///
    /// - Parameter request: The finding to put into words, and the one the user last disagreed with.
    func insightTools(for request: InsightRequest) -> [any Tool] {
        [CaffeineTimingTool(pattern: request.pattern, format: LanguageModelFormat(calendar: calendar))]
    }

    /// Answers one instruction in a new session, with new tools and the standing instructions, then discards the
    /// session.
    ///
    /// Nothing the model reads or writes is logged (constitution Article XI.6). A failure is logged with its error's
    /// domain and code only.
    func respond(to instruction: LanguageModelInstruction) async throws -> String {
        let session = LanguageModelSession(
            tools: tools(for: instruction.origin),
            instructions: LanguageModelInstructions.text(for: instruction.origin, locale: locale))
        do {
            return try await session.respond(to: instruction.prompt).content
        } catch {
            let domain = (error as NSError).domain
            let code = (error as NSError).code
            Self.logger.error(
                "Couldn't respond to an instruction: \(domain, privacy: .public) \(code, privacy: .public)")
            throw error
        }
    }

    /// Writes the Insights tab's first finding in a new session, with only `compareCaffeineTiming` and the insight
    /// instructions, through guided generation, then discards the session.
    ///
    /// Nothing the model reads or writes is logged (constitution Article XI.6). A failure is logged with its error's
    /// domain and code only.
    func writeInsight(_ request: InsightRequest) async throws -> WrittenInsight {
        let tools = insightTools(for: request)
        let session = LanguageModelSession(
            tools: tools, instructions: LanguageModelInstructions.insightText(locale: locale))
        do {
            let answer = try await session.respond(
                to: LanguageModelInstructions.insightPrompt(disagreement: request.disagreement),
                generating: GeneratedInsight.self)
            let facts = tools.compactMap { ($0 as? CaffeineTimingTool)?.facts }.joined(separator: "\n")
            return WrittenInsight(insight: answer.content.insight, facts: facts)
        } catch {
            let domain = (error as NSError).domain
            let code = (error as NSError).code
            Self.logger.error("Couldn't write an insight: \(domain, privacy: .public) \(code, privacy: .public)")
            throw error
        }
    }
}

```
    /// The facts an insight's prompt gives the model: the request's finding, with every number formatted (LMSRC-6).
    ///
    /// - Parameter request: The finding to put into words, and the one the user last disagreed with.
    func insightFacts(for request: InsightRequest) -> String {
        InsightFacts(pattern: request.pattern, format: LanguageModelFormat(calendar: calendar)).text
    }

    /// A new session for the Insights tab's first finding: the insight's instructions, and no tools, so the model has
    /// nothing to call again and again (LMSRC-6).
    func insightSession() -> LanguageModelSession {
        LanguageModelSession(instructions: LanguageModelInstructions.insightText(locale: locale))
    }

    /// Answers one instruction in a new session, with new tools and the standing instructions, then discards the
    /// session.
    ///
    /// Nothing the model reads or writes is logged (constitution Article XI.6). A failure is logged with its error's
    /// kind, domain, and code only (LMSRC-8).
    func respond(to instruction: LanguageModelInstruction) async throws -> String {
        let session = LanguageModelSession(
            tools: tools(for: instruction.origin),
            instructions: LanguageModelInstructions.text(for: instruction.origin, locale: locale))
        do {
            return try await session.respond(to: instruction.prompt).content
        } catch {
            let kind = Self.failureKind(of: error)
            let domain = (error as NSError).domain
            let code = (error as NSError).code
            Self.logger.error(
                """
                Couldn't respond to an instruction: \(kind, privacy: .public) \(domain, privacy: .public) \
                \(code, privacy: .public)
                """)
            throw error
        }
    }

    /// Writes the Insights tab's first finding in a new session with no tools, from a prompt that gives the facts,
    /// through guided generation capped at 150 tokens, then discards the session.
    ///
    /// Nothing the model reads or writes is logged (constitution Article XI.6). A failure is logged with its error's
    /// kind, domain, and code only (LMSRC-8).
    func writeInsight(_ request: InsightRequest) async throws -> WrittenInsight {
        let facts = insightFacts(for: request)
        let session = insightSession()
        do {
            let answer = try await session.respond(
                to: LanguageModelInstructions.insightPrompt(facts: facts, disagreement: request.disagreement),
                generating: GeneratedInsight.self, options: Self.insightOptions)
            return WrittenInsight(insight: answer.content.insight, facts: facts)
        } catch {
            let kind = Self.failureKind(of: error)
            let domain = (error as NSError).domain
            let code = (error as NSError).code
            Self.logger.error(
                """
                Couldn't write an insight: \(kind, privacy: .public) \(domain, privacy: .public) \
                \(code, privacy: .public)
                """)
            throw error
        }
    }

    /// The kind of error a session failed with, for the log: the name of the framework's `GenerationError` case,
    /// `toolCallError` when a tool threw, or `other` (LMSRC-8).
    ///
    /// It names the kind only. The error's description and context can hold the prompt, so they're never logged
    /// (constitution Article XI.6).
    ///
    /// - Parameter error: The error the session threw.
    static func failureKind(of error: any Error) -> String {
        switch error {
        case let error as LanguageModelSession.GenerationError: generationErrorKind(error)
        case is LanguageModelSession.ToolCallError: "toolCallError"
        default: "other"
        }
    }

    private static func generationErrorKind(_ error: LanguageModelSession.GenerationError) -> String {
        switch error {
        case .exceededContextWindowSize: "exceededContextWindowSize"
        case .assetsUnavailable: "assetsUnavailable"
        case .guardrailViolation: "guardrailViolation"
        case .unsupportedGuide: "unsupportedGuide"
        case .unsupportedLanguageOrLocale: "unsupportedLanguageOrLocale"
        case .decodingFailure: "decodingFailure"
        case .rateLimited: "rateLimited"
        case .concurrentRequests: "concurrentRequests"
        case .refusal: "refusal"
        @unknown default: "unknownGenerationError"
        }
    }
}

### 17:14:39 · Human

> Let's refine the insight real quick. The sentenceshould explain what the heading actually means. For example, I received a title of "Caffeine over 35mg at bedtime" with a sentence of "An early sign: over 35 mg on 21 nights, 35 mg or under on 6 nights, an early sign based on 27 nights." The phrasing doesn't explain what the insight was. What we should be noticing is things like, it seems like you sleep better with 35mg or less caffeine in your system at bedtime.

### 17:15:47 · Claude

Timestamp for your request first. Then I'll make the change test-first, like the rest.

There's one wording point to flag. The finding only measures **time asleep**, not how well you slept. So I'll phrase it as "you tend to sleep **longer** with 35 mg or less in your body when you fall asleep", not "sleep better". "Better" would claim more than the data shows, and the brief's Honesty criterion is about exactly that. Tell me if you'd rather have "better".

The plan has three parts:
- **Facts:** the rules add a plain-language line that states the direction, e.g. "What it means: you tend to sleep longer on nights you fall asleep with 35 mg or less in your body." It's worded for each direction (shorter, longer, about the same). The rules work out what it means and the model only phrases it, so the model can't get the direction wrong.
- **Rules:** the headline must say what the finding means, and the sentence must explain the headline (the meaning, the confidence words, and the nights), not just list counts.
- **Guides:** `GeneratedInsight`'s field descriptions say the same thing. There's no example headline in them, because a small model could copy an example even when the direction is the opposite.

What I need next, all independent:
1. The timestamp.
2. Whether `GenerationSchema` is `Encodable`, so a test can check the guide text.
3. Whether any UI test checks the insight's wording.
4. The red test edits for the new facts line and the reworded rules.

  | Longer on the nights over the threshold | You tend to sleep longer on nights you fall asleep with more than 40 mg in your body. |

    /// What the direction means for the user's sleep, in plain words. It says "longer", not "better", because the
    /// finding compares time asleep, not how well the user slept.
    private func meaning(of threshold: String) -> String {
        switch pattern.direction {
        case .shorter: "you tend to sleep longer on nights you fall asleep with \(threshold) or less in your body"
        case .longer: "you tend to sleep longer on nights you fall asleep with more than \(threshold) in your body"
        case .aboutTheSame:
            "you sleep about as long whether or not you fall asleep with more than \(threshold) in your body"
        }
    }

/// It mirrors ``Insight``, which can't be `@Generable` because Domain doesn't import FoundationModels (LMSRC-6). Its
/// guides ask for a headline of at most 8 words that says what the finding means, and a sentence of at most 25 that
/// explains it (LMSRC-9). They give no example headline, because the model could copy it even when the finding's
/// direction is the other way.
@Generable(description: "The one finding on the user's Insights card.")
struct GeneratedInsight {
    /// What the finding means, in plain words, in at most 8 words.
    @Guide(description: "What the finding means, in plain words, in at most 8 words.")
    let headline: String
    /// One sentence, of at most 25 words, that explains the headline.
    @Guide(
        description: """
            One sentence of at most 25 words that explains the headline, with the confidence words and how many \
            nights it's based on.
            """)
    let sentence: String
