```
// Re-measures the refined insight: new rules, prompt, facts line, guides, and answers that explain the headline.
import Foundation
import FoundationModels

@Generable(description: "The one finding on the user's Insights card.")
struct GeneratedInsight {
    @Guide(description: "What the finding means, in plain words, in at most 8 words.")
    let headline: String
    @Guide(
        description: """
            One sentence of at most 25 words that explains the headline, with the confidence words and how many \
            nights it's based on.
            """)
    let sentence: String
}

let instructions = [
    "You write short answers for Half-Life, an app that shows how much caffeine is in the user's body and when "
        + "it wears off.",
    "Get every number from the facts. Never calculate, estimate, or invent one.",
    "Repeat amounts and days exactly as the facts give them.",
    "If the facts say something isn't available, say so plainly.",
    "Don't diagnose, and don't give medical advice.",
    "Answer in three sentences or fewer.",
    "Answer in French.",
    "You're writing the one finding on the user's Insights card, from the facts in the prompt.",
    "Write a headline of at most 8 words that says what the finding means, in plain words.",
    "Write one sentence of at most 25 words that explains the headline: what the facts' \"What it means\" line "
        + "says, the confidence words, and how many nights it's based on.",
    "Don't just list the counts of nights.",
    "Use the facts' confidence words exactly: \"an early sign\" or \"consistent so far\".",
    "Describe a pattern in the user's nights. Never say caffeine caused, cost, or took away sleep, and never say "
        + "how much sleep the user lost.",
    "Don't give advice, and don't tell the user what to do.",
    "If the user disagreed with an earlier finding, take a different approach from that one.",
].joined(separator: "\n")

let prompt = """
    Write today's finding for the user's Insights card: a headline that says what the facts mean for the \
    user's sleep, in plain words, and one sentence that explains it.

    The facts:
    Period: the last 30 days, Aug 14 to Sep 12.
    Caffeine when you fell asleep: over 35 mg on 21 nights, 35 mg or under on 6 nights.
    Nights counted: 27.
    Time asleep: shorter on the nights over 35 mg than on the others.
    What it means: you tend to sleep longer on nights you fall asleep with 35 mg or less in your body.
    Confidence: an early sign.
    3 nights aren't counted: no sleep was recorded, or no drinks were logged before them yet.

    The user disagreed with this earlier finding:
    Headline: "Caffeine over 35mg at bedtime"
    Sentence: "An early sign: over 35 mg on 21 nights, 35 mg or under on 6 nights, an early sign based on 27 nights."
    Take a different approach from the one in that finding.
    """

let answers: [(String, String)] = [
    ("en", #"{"headline":"You sleep longer with less caffeine at bedtime","sentence":"You tend to sleep longer on nights you fall asleep with 35 mg or less in your body, an early sign based on 27 nights."}"#),
    ("fr", #"{"headline":"Vous dormez plus longtemps avec moins de caféine","sentence":"Vous avez tendance à dormir plus longtemps les nuits où vous vous endormez avec 35 mg ou moins dans le corps, un premier signe sur 27 nuits."}"#),
    ("de", #"{"headline":"Mit weniger Koffein schlafen Sie länger","sentence":"Sie schlafen tendenziell länger in Nächten, in denen Sie mit 35 mg oder weniger im Körper einschlafen, ein erstes Anzeichen aus 27 Nächten."}"#),
    ("ja", #"{"headline":"カフェインが少ない夜はより長く眠れる傾向","sentence":"就寝時の体内カフェインが35 mg以下の夜は睡眠時間が長い傾向があり、27夜に基づく初期の兆候です。"}"#),
    ("pt-BR", #"{"headline":"Você dorme mais com menos cafeína à noite","sentence":"Você tende a dormir mais nas noites em que adormece com 35 mg ou menos no corpo, um primeiro sinal com base em 27 noites."}"#),
]

@main
struct Measure {
    static func main() async throws {
        let model = SystemLanguageModel.default
        for (label, answer) in answers {
            print("answer \(label):", try await model.tokenCount(for: answer))
        }
        print("instructions:", try await model.tokenCount(for: Instructions(instructions)))
        print("prompt:", try await model.tokenCount(for: prompt))
        print("schema:", try await model.tokenCount(for: GeneratedInsight.generationSchema))
        let entries: [Transcript.Entry] = [
            .instructions(Transcript.Instructions(
                segments: [.text(Transcript.TextSegment(content: instructions))], toolDefinitions: [])),
            .prompt(Transcript.Prompt(
                segments: [.text(Transcript.TextSegment(content: prompt))],
                responseFormat: Transcript.ResponseFormat(schema: GeneratedInsight.generationSchema))),
            .response(Transcript.Response(
                assetIDs: [], segments: [.text(Transcript.TextSegment(content: answers[1].1))])),
        ]
        let total = try await model.tokenCount(for: entries)
        print("refined insight transcript (disagreement, fr answer): \(total) / \(model.contextSize)")
    }
}

```

### 17:37:01 · Claude

I can't fix this from evidence yet. On my side, the refined session measures 763 tokens, and the Mac's simulator can count tokens but can't run the model because Apple Intelligence is off, so I can't reproduce the failure. The other checks are clean: Half-Life's own docs have **0 warnings**, the guide text is built as intended, and the full unit + UI run is still going.

What I can say:
- **The overflow happens while the model is writing its answer.** The version you ran before the refinement already had the 150-token cap and no tool, and it gave you a result. So the refinement changed how the model answers, not how big the prompt is.
- **Hitting the cap may be reported as a context-window error.** The SDK doesn't say what happens when an answer reaches `maximumResponseTokens`. If hitting the cap shows up as `exceededContextWindowSize`, that means the model rambles past about 110 words. If the cap isn't honored during guided generation, the rambling fills all 4,096 tokens. Either way, the model isn't finishing its sentence.
- **One suspect is the quotation marks in the new rule.** It says ``what the facts' "What it means" line says``. The answer is written as JSON strings, and quotation marks copied into a JSON string are a plausible way to derail it. That's a guess, not a finding.

To go further, I need two things from you:
1. **The exact log line** that starts with `Couldn't write an insight:`. It now names the kind of error. Also tell me whether it came from a device or a simulator, and whether that build included my 17:31 changes.
2. **How you'd like to proceed:**
   - **A. Reproduce it first (my recommendation).** Turn on Apple Intelligence on this Mac, and the simulator can run the real model. I'd run the exact session with sample data and see what the model generates when it fails. Then the fix is based on evidence.
   - **B. Harden it now, without reproducing.** Give both fields a `.pattern` regex that limits their length, so the model must close the string, and remove the quotation marks from the rule. This could fix it, but I haven't tested whether a length-limited pattern works well for text in other languages.
   - **C. Roll back part of the refinement.** Keep the new "What it means" line, which gives the model the meaning, but go back to the old rules and prompt, which were working.

I'll hold here for your answer. The full unit + UI run keeps going in the background meanwhile.

### 17:37:18 · Human

> Couldn't write an insight: refusal FoundationModels.LanguageModelSession.GenerationError 8

### 17:38:08 · Claude

The error is a **refusal** (`GenerationError` code 8), not a context-window overflow. The model itself declined to write the insight. That's the new error-kind logging doing its job: before, this line would have shown only a domain and a number.

That also explains the timing. The version that worked asked only for the finding. The refinement asks what the facts **mean for the user's sleep**, and adds "you tend to sleep longer…", a general claim about the user's health. Apple's on-device model is trained to decline requests that look like interpreting someone's health, and an insight that explains what the finding means reads like that. (Separately, the "Don't diagnose…" rule was already in the instructions.)

The fix keeps your goal, a headline and sentence that explain the finding, but frames it as describing the user's own records rather than interpreting their health:
- **The "What it means" line** becomes past tense and describes the user's own nights: "you slept longer on the nights you fell asleep with 35 mg or less of caffeine." It no longer says "you tend to sleep longer… in your body".
- **The prompt** asks for "a headline that says the pattern in the facts, in plain words", and drops "what the facts mean for the user's sleep".
- **A new rule** gives the context: "The facts come from the drinks the user logged and the sleep Apple Health recorded."

I can't run the model here, so your device is the test. If it's still refused, the `Refusal` error has an `explanation` you can read in the debugger. I won't log that, because it can quote the prompt's health data.

What I need next, all independent:
1. A timestamp.
2. The full unit + UI run stopped. It's testing code that's about to change, and it's using the simulator the red run needs. I'll run the coverage check once, on the final code.
3. The red test edits: the new facts lines, the prompt, and the new rule.

```
- **Refusals.** The model can refuse to write an insight. Its log line then names `refusal`, and the card stays hidden. On 2026-09-13 it refused a prompt that asked what the facts meant for the user's sleep, with a line that generalized about it, so the insight describes the user's own nights in the past tense instead (<doc:Insights>). The refusal's explanation can quote the prompt, so it's never logged.
- **The number check.** The data source returns the insight with the facts it gave the model,
```

```
    /// What the direction means for the user's sleep, in plain words. It says "longer", not "better", because the
    /// finding compares time asleep, not how well the user slept.
    private func meaning(of threshold: String) -> String {
        switch pattern.direction {
        case .shorter: "you tend to sleep longer on nights you fall asleep with \(threshold) or less in your body"
        case .longer: "you tend to sleep longer on nights you fall asleep with more than \(threshold) in your body"
        case .aboutTheSame:
            "you sleep about as long whether or not you fall asleep with more than \(threshold) in your body"
        }
    }
```

```
    /// What the direction means, in plain words, about the user's own nights, in the past tense.
    ///
    /// It says "longer", not "better", because the finding compares time asleep, not how well the user slept. It
    /// describes the nights rather than generalizing about the user's health, because the model refused a line that
    /// generalized ("you tend to sleep longer") on 2026-09-13.
    private func meaning(of threshold: String) -> String {
        switch pattern.direction {
        case .shorter: "you slept longer on the nights you fell asleep with \(threshold) or less of caffeine"
        case .longer: "you slept longer on the nights you fell asleep with more than \(threshold) of caffeine"
        case .aboutTheSame:
            "you slept about as long whether or not you fell asleep with more than \(threshold) of caffeine"
        }
    }
```

### 17:44:31 · Human

> Still getting a refusal. Let's work on the rewording here. We want the title to say something along the lines of "You get the most sleep when below 35 mg". Then the description would describe a bit of how the data supports that claim.

### 17:46:00 · Claude

That's the second blind rewording to be refused, and I can't see why. The model's refusal carries an `explanation`, but I can't read it: Apple Intelligence is off on this Mac, and I won't log it, because it can quote the prompt's health data. So I'd rather pin the wording down with you before another round trip to your device.

Your example suggests a sturdier design. **Let the rules write the headline, and have the model write only the supporting sentence.** The headline you want ("You get the most sleep when below 35 mg") is fully determined by the finding's direction and threshold, so there's no need to ask the model to make that claim. What worked before the refinement was exactly this: the model describing counts of nights. So:

- **The facts gain a computed headline**, worded for each direction:
  - fewer nights over the threshold means shorter sleep: *"You get more sleep at 35 mg or less"*
  - the reverse: *"You get more sleep above 35 mg"*
  - no difference: *"Your sleep is about the same either side of 35 mg"*

  I'd say "more" rather than "the most". There are only two groups of nights, so "most" claims a peak the data doesn't show.
- **The model uses that headline**, in the user's language, and writes one sentence on how the data supports it. For example: *"On the 6 nights you fell asleep with 35 mg or less, you slept longer than on the other 21, an early sign."*
- **The rules shrink to match**: use the facts' headline, write one sentence of how the nights support it, and use the confidence words exactly. The rules against causation and advice stay. I'd drop the "What it means" line and the "Apple Health" rule, because neither stopped the refusal and both add health framing.

The confidence words go in the sentence, so an "early sign" still reads as tentative even though the headline is stated plainly.

What I need next, all independent:
1. A timestamp.
2. The verification run stopped. It's testing wording that's about to change, and it's holding my simulator.
3. Your call on the design, and whether you can turn on Apple Intelligence on this Mac. If you can, I'd run the exact session in the simulator and read the refusal explanation myself before you test on the device.

```
    /// What the direction means, in plain words, about the user's own nights, in the past tense.
    ///
    /// It says "longer", not "better", because the finding compares time asleep, not how well the user slept. It
    /// describes the nights rather than generalizing about the user's health, because the model refused a line that
    /// generalized ("you tend to sleep longer") on 2026-09-13.
    private func meaning(of threshold: String) -> String {
        switch pattern.direction {
        case .shorter: "you slept longer on the nights you fell asleep with \(threshold) or less of caffeine"
        case .longer: "you slept longer on the nights you fell asleep with more than \(threshold) of caffeine"
        case .aboutTheSame:
            "you slept about as long whether or not you fell asleep with more than \(threshold) of caffeine"
        }
    }
```
- `new_string`:

```
    /// The headline the rules write for the finding's direction, which the model uses in the user's language.
    ///
    /// The model refused twice on 2026-09-13 while it was asked what the finding meant for the user's sleep, so the
    /// rules write the claim, and the model only says how the nights support it. It says "more", not "the most",
    /// because the finding compares only two groups of nights, and "more sleep", not "better sleep", because it
    /// compares time asleep.
    private func headline(for threshold: String) -> String {
        switch pattern.direction {
        case .shorter: "You get more sleep at \(threshold) or less"
        case .longer: "You get more sleep above \(threshold)"
        case .aboutTheSame: "Your sleep is about the same either side of \(threshold)"
        }
    }
```

```

### 2026-09-13 15:43 -0400 — Fix the Insights card's language model session

- **Started:** 2026-09-13 15:43 -0400
- **Ended:** VERIFY_END
- **Human:** Adam Ure
- **AI tool / model:** Claude Code / Claude Opus 5
- **Transcript:** The session `ccfc6117`'s file in `ai_transcripts/`, written at the next export (not exported yet).
- **Type:** change, debugging
- **Request:** Implement the audit's first three proposals for the Insights tab's first card: the facts in the prompt instead of the `compareCaffeineTiming` tool, a cap on the answer's tokens, and the error's kind in the failure log. Then refine the insight's wording.
- **Interactions:**
  - `15:43` The owner asked to implement proposals 1 to 3 from the audit (the entry above).
  - `17:15` The owner asked for a sentence that explains the headline. They had received "Caffeine over 35mg at bedtime", with a sentence that only listed counts, and gave "it seems like you sleep better with 35mg or less caffeine in your system at bedtime" as an example.
  - `17:24` The owner pointed out that the docs had the new instructions but the prompt was unchanged, and asked for the prompt to change too.
  - `17:35` The owner reported that the new result went beyond the context window.
  - `17:38` The owner shared the log line: `Couldn't write an insight: refusal FoundationModels.LanguageModelSession.GenerationError 8`. The model had refused; the context window wasn't the problem.
  - `~17:40` The prompt's first line was changed on disk, not by this session, to "a headline that says what the app has discovered from the data". Seen when an edit was rejected at 17:40; recorded at 17:42. The AI kept it and brought the tests and docs in line.
  - `17:46` The owner reported a refusal again, and asked for a headline like "You get the most sleep when below 35 mg", with a sentence on how the data supports it.
  - `17:47` Asked two questions, the owner chose to have the rules write the headline, and to test on the device rather than turn on Apple Intelligence on the Mac.
  - `17:49` The owner asked to put the instructions in without waiting for the red run to finish.
- **AI contribution:**
  - **The facts in the prompt (proposal 1).** `CaffeineTimingTool` became `InsightFacts`, a plain type that writes the finding as lines of text. An insight's session has no tools. Its prompt gives the facts, and the data source returns the same facts for the number check. The standing rules for an insight take every number from the facts instead of a tool. `SimulatedLanguageModelDataSource` uses `InsightFacts`.
  - **The cap (proposal 2).** `GenerationOptions(maximumResponseTokens: 150)` on the insight's answer. The longest valid answer measured was 74 tokens, in French.
  - **The error's kind (proposal 3).** `failureKind(of:)` names the `GenerationError` case, `toolCallError`, or `other`, and both failure logs carry it as `.public`, with the domain and code. It found the refusal at 17:38.
  - **Wording, round 1 (17:15).** A "What it means" line in the facts ("you tend to sleep longer…"), rules and guides asking for a headline that says what the finding means and a sentence that explains it, and a prompt asking what the facts mean for the user's sleep. The AI used "longer", not "better", because the finding compares time asleep. The model refused it.
  - **Mistake.** At 17:35 the AI took the owner's report at face value and reasoned about a runaway answer against the cap, before asking for the log line, which showed a refusal.
  - **Wording, round 2 (17:38).** The line in the past tense about the user's own nights ("you slept longer…"), a rule saying where the facts come from, and the prompt reworded, then changed by the owner. Also refused.
  - **Wording, round 3 (17:47).** The rules write the headline for each direction ("You get more sleep at 35 mg or less", "You get more sleep above 35 mg", "Your sleep is about the same either side of 35 mg"). The model uses it in the user's language, and writes one sentence on how the nights support it, with the confidence words. The "What it means" line and the Health rule were dropped. "More", not "the most", because only two groups of nights are compared.
  - **Measurements.** In an iOS 26.5 simulator with the framework's tokenizer: the session measured 695 tokens with the tool, 655 without, 763 after round 1, and 773 after round 2. Answers in the new styles measured 54 to 64 tokens in English, French, German, Japanese, and Portuguese. Round 3 wasn't re-measured.
  - **Docs.** The Language Model, Insights, and Architecture articles: the decision, the facts in the prompt, the cap, refusals, the context budget table, LMSRC-4 to LMSRC-9, and LMTOOL-10/11 renamed LMFACT-1/2.
- **Human changes:** Chose the facts in the prompt, the cap, and the error's kind (15:43). Asked for the sentence to explain the headline, and for the prompt to change with the instructions. Changed the prompt's first line at about 17:40. Chose the computed headline and device testing (17:47).
- **Files:** `Half-Life/Data/DataSources/FoundationModels/InsightFacts.swift` (added), `Half-Life/Data/DataSources/FoundationModels/Tools/CaffeineTimingTool.swift` (deleted), `Half-Life/Data/DataSources/FoundationModels/{FoundationModelLanguageModelDataSource,LanguageModelDataSource,LanguageModelInstructions,GeneratedInsight}.swift`, `Half-Life/App/UITesting/SimulatedLanguageModelDataSource.swift`, `Half-Life/Domain/BusinessRules/InsightNumberRule.swift` (comment), `Half-LifeTests/Data/{InsightLanguageModelTests,LanguageModelDataSourceTests,LiveLanguageModelRepositoryInsightTests}.swift`, `Half-LifeTests/App/SimulatedLanguageModelDataSourceTests.swift`, `Half-Life/Documentation.docc/{LanguageModel,Insights,Architecture}.md`, `ai_log.md`.
- **Verification:**
  VERIFY_RESULTS
- **Notes:**
  - Not committed.
  - Round 3 hasn't been tried against the real model. Apple Intelligence is off on the Mac, so no test here runs the model, and the owner is testing on the device.
  - If the model still refuses, the refusal's `explanation` says why. It isn't logged, because it can quote the prompt's health data.
  - The builds and tests ran in a scratch copy of the live tree, with a private scheme in the git-ignored `xcuserdata/` that runs only the unit tests. Other sessions' changes in progress broke the shared test build three times (`SettingsViewAccessibilityID.appVersion`, `AppVersion`, and bf's `timeSpan` tests), and one red was taken with bf's in-progress test file left out of the copy. Around 16:30 and again at 16:4x, the simulator failed to boot while 17 simulators were running; it was erased and rebooted.
  - The audit's other proposals for questions (a call budget, a cap on `getDrinksOnDay`, a length limit on the question, and a token budget test) are still to decide.
```

```sh
    "Use the facts' headline as the headline, in the user's language.",
    "Write one sentence of at most 25 words that says how the nights in the facts support the headline.",
    "Use the facts' confidence words exactly: \\"an early sign\\" or \\"consistent so far\\".",
'''
src = src.replace(old_rules, new_rules)
src = src.replace('''a headline that says the pattern in the facts, in \\
    plain words, and one sentence that explains it.''', '''the facts' headline, and one sentence that says \\
    how the data supports it.''')
src = src.replace("What it means: you slept longer on the nights you fell asleep with 35 mg or less of caffeine.",
                  "Headline: You get more sleep at 35 mg or less.")
src = src.replace('@Guide(description: "What the finding means, in plain words, in at most 8 words.")',
                  '@Guide(description: "The facts\' headline, in the user\'s language.")')
src = src.replace('''One sentence of at most 25 words that explains the headline, with the confidence words and how many \\
            nights it's based on.''', '''One sentence of at most 25 words that says how the nights in the facts support the headline, with the \\
            confidence words.''')
src = src.replace(
    '("en", #"{"headline":"You sleep longer with less caffeine at bedtime","sentence":"You tend to sleep longer on nights you fall asleep with 35 mg or less in your body, an early sign based on 27 nights."}"#)',
    '("en", #"{"headline":"You get more sleep at 35 mg or less","sentence":"On the 6 nights you fell asleep with 35 mg or less, you slept longer than on the other 21, an early sign."}"#)')
src = src.replace("reworded insight transcript", "round 3 insight transcript")
for needle in ["Use the facts' headline", "the facts' headline, and one sentence", "Headline: You get more sleep",
               "The facts' headline, in the user's language", "support the headline, with the", "round 3"]:
    assert needle in src, needle
assert "What it means" not in src and "Apple Health recorded" not in src
open("measure5.swift", "w").write(src)
print("measure5.swift written")
EOF
```
